When the input expression is empty, `expression(s)` returns `ast.NameConstant(False)`. Wait, `ast.NameConstant` — but actually the issue is when the input is \"False\" itself.\n\nWait, let's trace: `Expression.compile(\"False\")`. The Scanner lexes \"False\" as an IDENT token (since \"False\" isn't \"or\"/\"and\"/\"not\"). Then `not_expr` creates `ast.Name(\"False\", ast.Load())`. Then `compile()` is called on that AST. In Python debug builds (3.8+), `compiler_nameop` asserts that the name isn't \"None\", \"True\", or \"False\" — because those should be constants, not names. So compiling `ast.Name(\"False\", ...)` crashes the interpreter in debug builds.\n\nThe fix (per bpo-40870): when the ident is \"True\", \"False\", or \"None\", create `ast.NameConstant` instead of `ast.Name`.\n\nLet me check how upstream pytest fixed this. The fix was in pytest commit — they map `True`/`False`/`None` to `ast.Constant`/`ast.NameConstant`. Let me look at the actual upstream fix.\n\nIn pytest, the fix (PR #9009, issue #8919?) Let me recall. The bug report: \"Pytest crashes the interpreter on debug build for 3.8+\" — this is pytest issue #8919?
